## Description

#### What type of PR is this?

/kind kep

#### What this PR does / why we need it:

Introduce the KEP for `waitForPodsReady.unscheduledTimeout`.

Adds [KEP-13502](keps/13502-unscheduled-pods-timeout/README.md), a design proposal for an optional Kueue configuration field that evicts and requeues admitted workloads when required pods remain unscheduled (`PodScheduled != True`) longer than a configured limit.

Today, `waitForPodsReady.timeout` is measured from workload admission and is often set high (e.g. 30m) to allow image pulls, init containers, and readiness probes after scheduling. That same window also applies to pods stuck in Pending, so transient scheduler or state inconsistencies can delay recovery.

This PR is **design only**. Implementation is tracked in [#13614](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13614) and will follow this KEP after merge.

The KEP proposes:

- **`waitForPodsReady.unscheduledTimeout`** — optional duration on `WaitForPodsReady` (config API v1beta1/v1beta2). Active only when set to a **positive** value; unset or `0s` disables enforcement and preserves current timeout behavior.
- **`PodsReady=False`, reason `WaitForScheduling`** — new workload condition reason (`WorkloadWaitForScheduling`) when required pods exist but are not all scheduled. Set for observability even when `unscheduledTimeout` is disabled.
- **`UnscheduledPodsTracker` controller** — central controller following the TopologyUngater pattern ([KEP-2724](keps/2724-topology-aware-scheduling/README.md), `ElasticJobUngater`). Reconciles per **Workload**, watches Pod events (batched), lists pods via the existing `WorkloadSliceNameKey` pod index (`WorkloadSliceNameAnnotation` / `WorkloadAnnotation`), checks `PodScheduled=True` on required pods, and sets `WaitForScheduling` vs delegates readiness to the job framework. Works out-of-the-box for custom in-house integrations without per-job code.
- **Job framework readiness only** — `GenericJob.PodsReady(ctx, client) bool` continues to evaluate pod readiness; scheduling detection is **not** implemented per integration in the primary design.
- **Dual-timeout behavior** — when `unscheduledTimeout` is enabled:
  - While reason is `WaitForScheduling`: enforce `unscheduledTimeout` from admission.
  - After all pods are scheduled (`WaitForStart`): enforce `timeout` from the `PodsReady` transition time (reconciliation observation time).
- **Eviction and requeue** — reuse `WorkloadEvictedByPodsReadyTimeout` with underlying cause `WorkloadWaitForScheduling` and the existing `requeuingStrategy` (no new eviction reason or requeue policy).
- **Validation** — `unscheduledTimeout` must be non-negative; when positive, it must not exceed `timeout`.
- **No feature gate** — behavior is controlled by configuration only.
- **Alternatives** — per-integration `PodsScheduled(ctx, client) (bool, error)` on `GenericJob` (prototyped in [#13614](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13614)) is documented under [Alternatives](keps/13502-unscheduled-pods-timeout/README.md#alternatives), not chosen as the primary mechanism.

**AI disclosure:** AI tools were used to evaluate various approaches during drafting. I've reviewed and finalised the content.

#### Which issue(s) this PR fixes:

Part of #13502

#### Special notes for your reviewer:

- KEP-only PR per maintainer guidance on [#13614](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13614).
- Mechanism updated per review feedback: primary design is TopologyUngater-style `UnscheduledPodsTracker`; per-integration `PodsScheduled` moved to Alternatives.
- Related KEPs: [349-all-or-nothing](keps/349-all-or-nothing), [1282-pods-ready-requeue-strategy](keps/1282-pods-ready-requeue-strategy).
- KEP milestone metadata targets **v0.20** (`kep.yaml`).
- **Recovery precedence:** if a running workload loses a scheduled pod, `WaitForRecovery` / `recoveryTimeout` wins over `WaitForScheduling` (documented in the KEP).
- **Backward compatibility:** when `unscheduledTimeout` is unset, eviction timing is unchanged; `WaitForScheduling` is still a new observable `PodsReady` reason consumers should tolerate.
- Proposed timeout interaction:

  | `PodsReady` reason | `unscheduledTimeout` | Timer start | Duration |
  |--------------------|----------------------|-------------|----------|
  | `WaitForScheduling` | not set / `0s` | Admission | `timeout` (unchanged) |
  | `WaitForScheduling` | set (> 0) | Admission | `unscheduledTimeout` |
  | `WaitForStart` | set (> 0) | `PodsReady.LastTransitionTime` | `timeout` |
  | `WaitForStart` | not set / `0s` | Admission | `timeout` (unchanged) |
  | `WaitForRecovery` | any | `PodsReady.LastTransitionTime` | `recoveryTimeout` (unchanged) |

- Planned test coverage in the implementation PR: `UnscheduledPodsTracker` unit tests, workload-controller timeout math, integration tests (Job + representative operator), `WaitForRecovery` precedence, list/index error handling, and regression of existing `waitForPodsReady` behavior with `unscheduledTimeout` omitted.

#### Does this PR introduce a user-facing change?

```release-note
NONE
```

## Summary by CodeRabbit

* **New Features**
  * Proposed an optional timeout for admitted workloads whose required pods remain unscheduled.
  * Added status indicators for scheduled pods and workloads awaiting required pods.
  * Defined recovery behavior after scheduling or observation failures, including stale scheduling conditions.
  * Clarified timeout, eviction, replacement, and requeue behavior, including explicit scheduling-wait outcomes.
  * Preserves existing behavior when the option is unset or zero.

* **Documentation**
  * Added the KEP proposal with validation details, compatibility guarantees, testing requirements, feature-gate metadata, and related metrics.

## Walkthrough

This change updates KEP-13502 with required-pod tracking rules, scheduling condition semantics, recovery behavior, timeout ownership, expanded tests, and provisional KEP metadata.

### Changes

**Unscheduled Pods Timeout**

|Layer / File(s)|Summary|
|---|---|
|**Tracking and condition semantics** <br> `keps/13502-unscheduled-pods-timeout/README.md`|The KEP excludes terminated pods from active counts, requires non-terminated replacements, defines `PodsScheduled` status entries, and documents stale conditions after observation failures.|
|**Timeout and recovery flow** <br> `keps/13502-unscheduled-pods-timeout/README.md`|The initial path waits for `PodsScheduled=True`. The recovery path evaluates `PodsReady` when scheduling is false. The workload controller selects timeout causes and eviction ownership.|
|**Validation, tests, and KEP metadata** <br> `keps/13502-unscheduled-pods-timeout/README.md`, `keps/13502-unscheduled-pods-timeout/kep.yaml`|The KEP expands tracker, controller, and recovery-path tests. It adds ownership, lifecycle, feature-gate, milestone, and metric metadata.|

**Estimated code review effort:** 2 (Simple) | ~10 minutes

<!-- final_review_risk_start -->
**Merge Risk:** _🟡 Moderate_ · up to `e0a1a`

The proposed timeout and workload-status behavior still has unresolved correctness gaps around recovery transitions, stale scheduling state, validation, status contracts, and controller ownership. These could lead to incorrect evictions, requeues, or observable workload conditions when implemented, so the KEP is not merge-ready until the contracts are clarified.
<!-- final_review_risk_end -->

**Suggested reviewers:** `mimowo`, `pbundyra`, `tenzen-y`

+# KEP-13502: Unscheduled Pods Timeout
+
+<!-- toc -->
+- [Summary](#summary)
+- [Motivation](#motivation)
+  - [Goals](#goals)
+  - [Non-Goals](#non-goals)
+- [Proposal](#proposal)
+  - [User Stories](#user-stories)
+    - [Story 1](#story-1)
+- [Design Details](#design-details)
+  - [Kueue Configuration API](#kueue-configuration-api)
+  - [Workload PodsReady condition](#workload-podsready-condition)
+  - [Timeout interaction](#timeout-interaction)
+  - [Eviction and requeue](#eviction-and-requeue)
+  - [Test Plan](#test-plan)
+  - [Backward compatibility](#backward-compatibility)
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)
+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+Add `waitForPodsReady.unscheduledTimeout` to detect admitted workloads whose pods remain
+unscheduled (`PodScheduled != True`) sooner than the existing `timeout`, which is intended
+for post-schedule startup (image pull, init containers, readiness probes).
+
+## Motivation
+
+On on-premises clusters, pods may take a long time to become Ready after scheduling because
+of image pulling or initialization. Operators often set `waitForPodsReady.timeout` to 30
+minutes to accommodate that.
+
+However, pods that remain unscheduled due to timing or transient state inconsistencies
+between Kueue and the scheduler should be detected and requeued sooner. A separate timeout
+allows faster recovery without shortening the time allowed for normal pod startup.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Add a configurable timeout for admitted workloads with unscheduled pods.
+- Evict and requeue workloads that exceed the unscheduled timeout, reusing the existing
+  `waitForPodsReady` eviction and requeue machinery.
+- When `unscheduledTimeout` is enabled, start the `timeout` clock when all pods are scheduled,
+  so startup time is not consumed by scheduling delays.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Replacing or modifying kube-scheduler scheduling queue timeouts.
+- Changing MultiKueue `PodScheduled` status sync behavior.
+- A separate eviction reason or requeue strategy; existing `PodsReadyTimeout` and
+  `requeuingStrategy` are reused.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Extend `WaitForPodsReady` with an optional `unscheduledTimeout` field. Job controllers
+distinguish unscheduled pods from scheduled-but-not-ready pods via a new `PodsReady`
+condition reason `WaitForScheduling`. The workload controller enforces the shorter timeout
+when that reason is active and `unscheduledTimeout` is configured.
+
+### User Stories
+
+#### Story 1
+
+An operator configures `waitForPodsReady.timeout: 30m` and `unscheduledTimeout: 5m`.
+A workload is admitted but its pods remain Pending because of a transient scheduler glitch.
+After 5 minutes Kueue evicts and requeues the workload. A pod that is scheduled but still
+pulling an image is allowed the full 30 minutes from the moment all pods are scheduled.
+
+## Design Details
+
+### Kueue Configuration API
+
+```yaml
+waitForPodsReady:
+  timeout: 30m
+  unscheduledTimeout: 5m
+```
+
+```go
+type WaitForPodsReady struct {
+    // ...
+    // UnscheduledTimeout defines the time for an admitted workload to have all
+    // required pods reach PodScheduled=True. When exceeded, the workload is evicted
+    // and requeued. Must be non-negative and must not exceed timeout.
+    // Defaults to disabled when unset or "0s".
+    // +optional
+    UnscheduledTimeout *metav1.Duration `json:"unscheduledTimeout,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+### Workload PodsReady condition
+
+Add constant `WorkloadWaitForScheduling = "WaitForScheduling"`.
+
+Job controllers implement `PodsScheduled(ctx, client) (bool, error)` on the `GenericJob` interface.
+`generatePodsReadyCondition` sets `PodsReady=False` with reason `WaitForScheduling` when
+not all required pods have `PodScheduled=True`.
```

  **Define the `PodsScheduled` contract before implementation.**
  
  The KEP proposes `PodsScheduled(ctx, client) (bool, error)`, but `GenericJob` still defines only `PodsReady(ctx, client) bool`, and the workload timeout selector maps `WaitForScheduling` to `WorkloadWaitForStart`. Define required pods for missing, deleted, replacement, succeeded, replacement, and optional workloads; define the fallback for integrations without scheduling support; and specify handling for `PodsScheduled` errors so status updates cannot evict a healthy workload or requeue incorrectly.

  incorrectly. Align the GenericJob PodsScheduled signature and the

+
+## Summary
+
+Add `waitForPodsReady.unscheduledTimeout` to detect admitted workloads whose pods remain
+unscheduled (`PodScheduled != True`) sooner than the existing `timeout`, which is intended
+for post-schedule startup (image pull, init containers, readiness probes).
+
+## Motivation
+
+On on-premises clusters, pods may take a long time to become Ready after scheduling because
+of image pulling or initialization. Operators often set `waitForPodsReady.timeout` to 30
+minutes to accommodate that.
+

+between Kueue and the scheduler should be detected and requeued sooner. A separate timeout
+allows faster recovery without shortening the time allowed for normal pod startup.
+
+### Goals

+- Add a configurable timeout for admitted workloads with unscheduled pods.
+- Evict and requeue workloads that exceed the unscheduled timeout, reusing the existing
+  `waitForPodsReady` eviction and requeue machinery.
+- When `unscheduledTimeout` is enabled, start the `timeout` clock when all pods are scheduled,
+  so startup time is not consumed by scheduling delays.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Replacing or modifying kube-scheduler scheduling queue timeouts.
+- Changing MultiKueue `PodScheduled` status sync behavior.
+- A separate eviction reason or requeue strategy; existing `PodsReadyTimeout` and
+  `requeuingStrategy` are reused.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Extend `WaitForPodsReady` with an optional `unscheduledTimeout` field. Job controllers
+distinguish unscheduled pods from scheduled-but-not-ready pods via a new `PodsReady`
+condition reason `WaitForScheduling`. The workload controller enforces the shorter timeout
+when that reason is active and `unscheduledTimeout` is configured.
+
+### User Stories
+
+#### Story 1
+
+An operator configures `waitForPodsReady.timeout: 30m` and `unscheduledTimeout: 5m`.

+After 5 minutes Kueue evicts and requeues the workload. A pod that is scheduled but still
+pulling an image is allowed the full 30 minutes from the moment all pods are scheduled.
+
+## Design Details
+
+### Kueue Configuration API
+
+```yaml
+waitForPodsReady:
+  timeout: 30m
+  unscheduledTimeout: 5m
+```
+
+```go
+type WaitForPodsReady struct {
+    // ...
+    // UnscheduledTimeout defines the time for an admitted workload to have all
+    // required pods reach PodScheduled=True. When exceeded, the workload is evicted
+    // and requeued. Must be non-negative and must not exceed timeout.
+    // Defaults to disabled when unset or "0s".
+    // +optional
+    UnscheduledTimeout *metav1.Duration `json:"unscheduledTimeout,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+### Workload PodsReady condition
+
+Add constant `WorkloadWaitForScheduling = "WaitForScheduling"`.
+
+Job controllers implement `PodsScheduled(ctx, client) (bool, error)` on the `GenericJob` interface.
+`generatePodsReadyCondition` sets `PodsReady=False` with reason `WaitForScheduling` when
+not all required pods have `PodScheduled=True`.
+
+### Timeout interaction
+
+| `PodsReady` reason | `unscheduledTimeout` | Timer start | Duration |
+|--------------------|----------------------|-------------|----------|
+| `WaitForScheduling` | not set / `0s` | `WorkloadAdmitted` | `timeout` (backward compatible) |
+| `WaitForScheduling` | set | `WorkloadAdmitted` | `unscheduledTimeout` |
+| `WaitForStart` | set | `PodsReady.LastTransitionTime` | `timeout` |
```

  **Clarify the startup timeout clock source.**
  
  The plan says jobs should supply scheduling information, but `GenericJob.PodsReady` still returns only `bool`. `generatePodsReadyCondition` then uses the reconciliation clock when setting `PodsReady.LastTransitionTime`, so the table entry “`PodsReady.LastTransitionTime`” records the first observed transition, not the last pod’s `PodScheduled=True` transition.
  
  If delayed reconciles are intended to grant extra startup time, document that observation-time semantics. If not, pass the source `PodScheduled=True` time into timeout duration calculation.
  
  <details>
  <summary>🤖 Prompt for AI Agents</summary>
  
  ```
  Verify each finding against current code. Fix only still-valid issues, skip the
  rest with a brief reason, keep changes minimal, and validate.
  
  In `@keps/13502-unscheduled-pods-timeout/README.md` around lines 100 - 104,
  Clarify the `WaitForStart` row in the timeout table to define whether
  `PodsReady.LastTransitionTime` uses reconciliation observation time or the
  latest pod `PodScheduled=True` transition. If observation-time semantics are
  intended, document that delayed reconciliation can extend startup time;
  otherwise update the `GenericJob.PodsReady`/`generatePodsReadyCondition` flow to
  propagate the source transition timestamp into timeout calculation.
  ```
  
  </details>

+  `requeuingStrategy` are reused.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Extend `WaitForPodsReady` with an optional `unscheduledTimeout` field. Job controllers
+distinguish unscheduled pods from scheduled-but-not-ready pods via a new `PodsReady`
+condition reason `WaitForScheduling`. The workload controller enforces the shorter timeout
+when that reason is active and `unscheduledTimeout` is configured.
+
+### User Stories
+
+#### Story 1
+
+An operator configures `waitForPodsReady.timeout: 30m` and `unscheduledTimeout: 5m`.
+A workload is admitted but its pods remain Pending because of a transient scheduler glitch.
+After 5 minutes Kueue evicts and requeues the workload. A pod that is scheduled but still
+pulling an image is allowed the full 30 minutes from the moment all pods are scheduled.
+
+## Design Details
+
+### Kueue Configuration API
+
+```yaml
+waitForPodsReady:
+  timeout: 30m
+  unscheduledTimeout: 5m
+```
+
+```go
+type WaitForPodsReady struct {
+    // ...
+    // UnscheduledTimeout defines the time for an admitted workload to have all
+    // required pods reach PodScheduled=True. When exceeded, the workload is evicted
+    // and requeued. Must be non-negative and must not exceed timeout.
+    // Defaults to disabled when unset or "0s".
+    // +optional
+    UnscheduledTimeout *metav1.Duration `json:"unscheduledTimeout,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+### Workload PodsReady condition
+
+Add constant `WorkloadWaitForScheduling = "WaitForScheduling"`.
+
+Job controllers implement `PodsScheduled(ctx, client) (bool, error)` on the `GenericJob` interface.
+`generatePodsReadyCondition` sets `PodsReady=False` with reason `WaitForScheduling` when
+not all required pods have `PodScheduled=True`.
+
+### Timeout interaction
+
+| `PodsReady` reason | `unscheduledTimeout` | Timer start | Duration |
+|--------------------|----------------------|-------------|----------|
+| `WaitForScheduling` | not set / `0s` | `WorkloadAdmitted` | `timeout` (backward compatible) |
+| `WaitForScheduling` | set | `WorkloadAdmitted` | `unscheduledTimeout` |
+| `WaitForStart` | set | `PodsReady.LastTransitionTime` | `timeout` |
+| `WaitForStart` | not set | `WorkloadAdmitted` | `timeout` (unchanged) |
+| `WaitForRecovery` | any | `PodsReady.LastTransitionTime` | `recoveryTimeout` (unchanged) |
```

  **Define precedence for the running-workload unschedule case.**
  
  The KEP table says any `WaitForRecovery` reason uses `recoveryTimeout`, but the KEP also says `WaitForScheduling` applies when a required pod is unscheduled. A running workload whose scheduled pod is removed can go from `PodsReady=True` to `PodsReady=False`, and the reconciler currently keeps `WaitForRecovery`, so recovery timing wins. State whether recovery timeout applies for deleted/preempted workloads; if not, update `generatePodsReadyCondition` and add an integration test for this transition.

@@ -0,0 +1,130 @@
+# KEP-13502: Unscheduled Pods Timeout
+
+<!-- toc -->
+- [Summary](#summary)
+- [Motivation](#motivation)
+  - [Goals](#goals)
+  - [Non-Goals](#non-goals)
+- [Proposal](#proposal)
+  - [User Stories](#user-stories)
+    - [Story 1](#story-1)
+- [Design Details](#design-details)
+  - [Kueue Configuration API](#kueue-configuration-api)
+  - [Workload PodsReady condition](#workload-podsready-condition)
+  - [Timeout interaction](#timeout-interaction)
+  - [Eviction and requeue](#eviction-and-requeue)
+  - [Test Plan](#test-plan)
+  - [Backward compatibility](#backward-compatibility)
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)
+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+Add `waitForPodsReady.unscheduledTimeout` to detect admitted workloads whose pods remain
+unscheduled (`PodScheduled != True`) sooner than the existing `timeout`, which is intended
+for post-schedule startup (image pull, init containers, readiness probes).
+
+## Motivation
+
+On on-premises clusters, pods may take a long time to become Ready after scheduling because
+of image pulling or initialization. Operators often set `waitForPodsReady.timeout` to 30
+minutes to accommodate that.
+
+However, pods that remain unscheduled due to timing or transient state inconsistencies
+between Kueue and the scheduler should be detected and requeued sooner. A separate timeout
+allows faster recovery without shortening the time allowed for normal pod startup.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Add a configurable timeout for admitted workloads with unscheduled pods.
+- Evict and requeue workloads that exceed the unscheduled timeout, reusing the existing
+  `waitForPodsReady` eviction and requeue machinery.
+- When `unscheduledTimeout` is enabled, start the `timeout` clock when all pods are scheduled,
+  so startup time is not consumed by scheduling delays.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Replacing or modifying kube-scheduler scheduling queue timeouts.
+- Changing MultiKueue `PodScheduled` status sync behavior.
+- A separate eviction reason or requeue strategy; existing `PodsReadyTimeout` and
+  `requeuingStrategy` are reused.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Extend `WaitForPodsReady` with an optional `unscheduledTimeout` field. Job controllers
+distinguish unscheduled pods from scheduled-but-not-ready pods via a new `PodsReady`
+condition reason `WaitForScheduling`. The workload controller enforces the shorter timeout
+when that reason is active and `unscheduledTimeout` is configured.
+
+### User Stories
+
+#### Story 1
+
+An operator configures `waitForPodsReady.timeout: 30m` and `unscheduledTimeout: 5m`.
+A workload is admitted but its pods remain Pending because of a transient scheduler glitch.
+After 5 minutes Kueue evicts and requeues the workload. A pod that is scheduled but still
+pulling an image is allowed the full 30 minutes from the moment all pods are scheduled.
+
+## Design Details
+
+### Kueue Configuration API
+
+```yaml
+waitForPodsReady:
+  timeout: 30m
+  unscheduledTimeout: 5m
+```
+
+```go
+type WaitForPodsReady struct {
+    // ...
+    // UnscheduledTimeout defines the time for an admitted workload to have all
+    // required pods reach PodScheduled=True. When exceeded, the workload is evicted
+    // and requeued. Must be non-negative and must not exceed timeout.
+    // Defaults to disabled when unset or "0s".
+    // +optional
+    UnscheduledTimeout *metav1.Duration `json:"unscheduledTimeout,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+### Workload PodsReady condition
+
+Add constant `WorkloadWaitForScheduling = "WaitForScheduling"`.
+
+Job controllers implement `PodsScheduled(ctx, client) (bool, error)` on the `GenericJob` interface.
+`generatePodsReadyCondition` sets `PodsReady=False` with reason `WaitForScheduling` when
+not all required pods have `PodScheduled=True`.
+
+### Timeout interaction
+
+| `PodsReady` reason | `unscheduledTimeout` | Timer start | Duration |
+|--------------------|----------------------|-------------|----------|
+| `WaitForScheduling` | not set / `0s` | `WorkloadAdmitted` | `timeout` (backward compatible) |
+| `WaitForScheduling` | set | `WorkloadAdmitted` | `unscheduledTimeout` |
+| `WaitForStart` | set | `PodsReady.LastTransitionTime` | `timeout` |
+| `WaitForStart` | not set | `WorkloadAdmitted` | `timeout` (unchanged) |
+| `WaitForRecovery` | any | `PodsReady.LastTransitionTime` | `recoveryTimeout` (unchanged) |
+
+When `unscheduledTimeout` is not configured, `WaitForScheduling` is still set for
+observability but timeout math is unchanged from today.
```

@@ -0,0 +1,292 @@
+# KEP-13502: Unscheduled Pods Timeout
+
+<!-- toc -->
+- [Summary](#summary)
+- [Motivation](#motivation)
+  - [Goals](#goals)
+  - [Non-Goals](#non-goals)
+- [Proposal](#proposal)
+  - [User Stories (Optional)](#user-stories-optional)
+    - [Story 1](#story-1)
+  - [Notes/Constraints/Caveats (Optional)](#notesconstraintscaveats-optional)
+  - [Risks and Mitigations](#risks-and-mitigations)
+- [Design Details](#design-details)
+  - [Kueue Configuration API](#kueue-configuration-api)
+  - [UnscheduledPodsTracker controller](#unscheduledpodstracker-controller)
+  - [Workload PodsReady condition](#workload-podsready-condition)
+  - [Timeout interaction](#timeout-interaction)
+  - [Eviction and requeue](#eviction-and-requeue)
+  - [Test Plan](#test-plan)
+    - [Prerequisite testing updates](#prerequisite-testing-updates)
+    - [Unit tests](#unit-tests)
+    - [Integration tests](#integration-tests)
+    - [e2e tests](#e2e-tests)
+  - [Graduation Criteria](#graduation-criteria)
+  - [Backward compatibility](#backward-compatibility)
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)
+- [Drawbacks](#drawbacks)
+- [Alternatives](#alternatives)
+  - [Per-integration <code>PodsScheduled</code> on <code>GenericJob</code>](#per-integration-podsscheduled-on-genericjob)
+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+Add `waitForPodsReady.unscheduledTimeout` to detect admitted workloads whose pods remain
+unscheduled (`PodScheduled != True`) sooner than the existing `timeout`, which is intended
+for post-schedule startup (image pull, init containers, readiness probes).
+
+## Motivation
+
+On on-premises clusters, pods may take a long time to become Ready after scheduling because
+of image pulling or initialization. Operators often set `waitForPodsReady.timeout` to 30
+minutes to accommodate that.
+
+However, pods that remain unscheduled due to timing or transient state inconsistencies
+between Kueue and the scheduler should be detected and requeued sooner. A separate timeout
+allows faster recovery without shortening the time allowed for normal pod startup.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Add a configurable timeout for admitted workloads with unscheduled pods.
+- Evict and requeue workloads that exceed the unscheduled timeout, reusing the existing
+  `waitForPodsReady` eviction and requeue machinery.
+- When `unscheduledTimeout` is enabled, start the `timeout` clock when all pods are scheduled,
+  so startup time is not consumed by scheduling delays.
+- Detect scheduling state centrally so custom in-house job integrations work without
+  per-integration code.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Replacing or modifying kube-scheduler scheduling queue timeouts.
+- Changing MultiKueue `PodScheduled` status sync behavior.
+- A separate eviction reason or requeue strategy; existing `PodsReadyTimeout` and
+  `requeuingStrategy` are reused.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Extend `WaitForPodsReady` with an optional `unscheduledTimeout` field. A new
+`UnscheduledPodsTracker` controller (following the TopologyUngater pattern) lists pods per
+admitted Workload, distinguishes unscheduled pods from scheduled-but-not-ready pods, and sets
+a new `PodsReady` condition reason `WaitForScheduling`. The workload controller enforces the
+shorter timeout when that reason is active and `unscheduledTimeout` is configured.
+
+### User Stories (Optional)
+
+#### Story 1
+
+An operator configures `waitForPodsReady.timeout: 30m` and `unscheduledTimeout: 5m`.
+A workload is admitted but its pods remain Pending because of a transient scheduler glitch.
+After 5 minutes Kueue evicts and requeues the workload. A pod that is scheduled but still
+pulling an image is allowed the full 30 minutes from the moment all pods are scheduled.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats (Optional)
+
+- `unscheduledTimeout` does not change kube-scheduler behavior or MultiKueue `PodScheduled`
+  status sync.
+- For `WaitForStart` when `unscheduledTimeout` is set, the startup timer uses reconciliation
+  observation time (`PodsReady.LastTransitionTime` when the reason changes), not the latest
+  pod `PodScheduled=True` transition.
+- When a running workload loses a scheduled pod, `WaitForRecovery` and `recoveryTimeout`
+  take precedence over `WaitForScheduling`.
+- `UnscheduledPodsTracker` and the job framework must coordinate updates to the `PodsReady`
+  condition so scheduling and readiness state do not conflict.
```

  🌐 Web query:
  
  `Kubernetes enhancements PR 13614 unscheduled pods timeout UnscheduledPodsTracker PodsReady WaitForRecovery`
  
  💡 Result:
  
  The PR 13614 in the Kubernetes-sigs Kueue repository introduces a feature to add an optional `waitForPodsReady.unscheduledTimeout` configuration field [1]. This enhancement is designed to improve the "all-or-nothing" scheduling semantics provided by the `waitForPodsReady` feature [2]. Previously, the general `waitForPodsReady.timeout` was applied from the moment of workload admission [1]. This often forced users to set long timeouts (e.g., 30 minutes) to accommodate image pulls and init containers, which unfortunately also allowed pods stuck in a `Pending` state (due to scheduling issues) to hold onto cluster resources for the entire duration [1]. Key aspects of this change include: * Unscheduled Timeout: When `unscheduledTimeout` is configured, the workload controller enforces this shorter limit specifically for pods that remain unscheduled (`PodScheduled != True`) from the time of admission [1]. * Decoupled Timers: Once all pods are successfully scheduled, the primary `waitForPodsReady.timeout` begins, governing the transition to `WaitForStart` and ensuring pods reach a `Ready` state [1]. * Observability and Recovery: If the `unscheduledTimeout` is exceeded, the workload is evicted and requeued, using the `WorkloadEvictedByPodsReadyTimeout` reason with the specific cause `WorkloadWaitForScheduling` [1]. If `unscheduledTimeout` is unset or set to 0, the existing behavior is preserved [1]. This PR also includes updates to job framework integration, API validation, and documentation to support this new configuration field [1]. It was created to address KEP-13502 [1][3]. Regarding the broader context of pod timeouts in Kubernetes: * `WaitForPodsReady` is a Kueue feature, not part of core Kubernetes scheduling [2][4]. * Core Kubernetes scheduler logic related to unschedulable pods (often involving a 5-minute timeout for pods in the unschedulable queue) is managed via mechanisms like `pod-max-in-unschedulable-pods-duration` [5] or event-based scheduling updates [6]. * Recent developments in the core scheduler (such as PR 137607) have focused on improving how DRA (Dynamic Resource Allocation) filter timeouts are handled by returning an `Error` rather than `UnschedulableAndUnresolvable` to ensure automatic retries from the backoff queue [7]. Top results: [1][3][6][2][4]

  **Align the KEP with the implementation’s condition owner.**
  
  The KEP assigns scheduling state to `UnscheduledPodsTracker` and excludes per-integration scheduling probes. ```diff
@@ -0,0 +1,292 @@
+# KEP-13502: Unscheduled Pods Timeout
+
+<!-- toc -->
+- [Summary](#summary)
+- [Motivation](#motivation)
+  - [Goals](#goals)
+  - [Non-Goals](#non-goals)
+- [Proposal](#proposal)
+  - [User Stories (Optional)](#user-stories-optional)
+    - [Story 1](#story-1)
+  - [Notes/Constraints/Caveats (Optional)](#notesconstraintscaveats-optional)
+  - [Risks and Mitigations](#risks-and-mitigations)
+- [Design Details](#design-details)
+  - [Kueue Configuration API](#kueue-configuration-api)
+  - [UnscheduledPodsTracker controller](#unscheduledpodstracker-controller)
+  - [Workload PodsReady condition](#workload-podsready-condition)
+  - [Timeout interaction](#timeout-interaction)
+  - [Eviction and requeue](#eviction-and-requeue)
+  - [Test Plan](#test-plan)
+    - [Prerequisite testing updates](#prerequisite-testing-updates)
+    - [Unit tests](#unit-tests)
+    - [Integration tests](#integration-tests)
+    - [e2e tests](#e2e-tests)
+  - [Graduation Criteria](#graduation-criteria)
+  - [Backward compatibility](#backward-compatibility)
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)
+- [Drawbacks](#drawbacks)
+- [Alternatives](#alternatives)
+  - [Per-integration <code>PodsScheduled</code> on <code>GenericJob</code>](#per-integration-podsscheduled-on-genericjob)
+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+Add `waitForPodsReady.unscheduledTimeout` to detect admitted workloads whose pods remain
+unscheduled (`PodScheduled != True`) sooner than the existing `timeout`, which is intended
+for post-schedule startup (image pull, init containers, readiness probes).
+
+## Motivation
+
+On on-premises clusters, pods may take a long time to become Ready after scheduling because
+of image pulling or initialization. Operators often set `waitForPodsReady.timeout` to 30
+minutes to accommodate that.
+
+However, pods that remain unscheduled due to timing or transient state inconsistencies
+between Kueue and the scheduler should be detected and requeued sooner. A separate timeout
+allows faster recovery without shortening the time allowed for normal pod startup.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Add a configurable timeout for admitted workloads with unscheduled pods.
+- Evict and requeue workloads that exceed the unscheduled timeout, reusing the existing
+  `waitForPodsReady` eviction and requeue machinery.
+- When `unscheduledTimeout` is enabled, start the `timeout` clock when all pods are scheduled,
+  so startup time is not consumed by scheduling delays.
+- Detect scheduling state centrally so custom in-house job integrations work without
+  per-integration code.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Replacing or modifying kube-scheduler scheduling queue timeouts.
+- Changing MultiKueue `PodScheduled` status sync behavior.
+- A separate eviction reason or requeue strategy; existing `PodsReadyTimeout` and
+  `requeuingStrategy` are reused.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Extend `WaitForPodsReady` with an optional `unscheduledTimeout` field. A new
+`UnscheduledPodsTracker` controller (following the TopologyUngater pattern) lists pods per
+admitted Workload, distinguishes unscheduled pods from scheduled-but-not-ready pods, and sets
+a new `PodsReady` condition reason `WaitForScheduling`. The workload controller enforces the
+shorter timeout when that reason is active and `unscheduledTimeout` is configured.
+
+### User Stories (Optional)
+
+#### Story 1
+
+An operator configures `waitForPodsReady.timeout: 30m` and `unscheduledTimeout: 5m`.
+A workload is admitted but its pods remain Pending because of a transient scheduler glitch.
+After 5 minutes Kueue evicts and requeues the workload. A pod that is scheduled but still
+pulling an image is allowed the full 30 minutes from the moment all pods are scheduled.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats (Optional)
+
+- `unscheduledTimeout` does not change kube-scheduler behavior or MultiKueue `PodScheduled`
+  status sync.
+- For `WaitForStart` when `unscheduledTimeout` is set, the startup timer uses reconciliation
+  observation time (`PodsReady.LastTransitionTime` when the reason changes), not the latest
+  pod `PodScheduled=True` transition.
+- When a running workload loses a scheduled pod, `WaitForRecovery` and `recoveryTimeout`
+  take precedence over `WaitForScheduling`.
+- `UnscheduledPodsTracker` and the job framework must coordinate updates to the `PodsReady`
+  condition so scheduling and readiness state do not conflict.
+
+### Risks and Mitigations
+
+| Risk | Mitigation |
+|------|------------|
+| Evict a healthy workload on a transient list/index error | Do not set `WaitForScheduling` or evict on client errors; retry on the next reconcile |
+| Increased requeue churn when `unscheduledTimeout` is too low | Operator tuning; existing `requeuingStrategy` backoff |
+| Consumers break on the new `WaitForScheduling` reason | Document in backward compatibility; reason is set even when `unscheduledTimeout` is disabled |
+| Controller and job framework both update `PodsReady` | Clear ownership: tracker reports scheduling; job framework reports readiness |
+
+## Design Details
+
+### Kueue Configuration API
+
+```yaml
+waitForPodsReady:
+  timeout: 30m
+  unscheduledTimeout: 5m
+```
+
+```go
+type WaitForPodsReady struct {
+    // ...
+    // UnscheduledTimeout defines the time for an admitted workload to have all
+    // required pods reach PodScheduled=True. When exceeded, the workload is evicted
+    // and requeued. Must be non-negative and must not exceed timeout.
+    // Defaults to disabled when unset or "0s".
+    // +optional
+    UnscheduledTimeout *metav1.Duration `json:"unscheduledTimeout,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+### UnscheduledPodsTracker controller
+
+A new controller, `UnscheduledPodsTracker`, follows the same architectural pattern as
+`TopologyUngater` ([KEP-2724](keps/2724-topology-aware-scheduling/README.md)) and
+`ElasticJobUngater`:
+
+- Reconciles per **Workload** (not per Job integration).
+- Watches **Pod** create/update/delete events and batches reconcile requests (using
+  `UpdatesBatchPeriod`, as TopologyUngater does).
+- Lists pods for a workload using the existing pod field index
+  (`WorkloadSliceNameKey` / `IndexPodWorkloadSliceName` in
+  `pkg/controller/core/indexer`), which indexes by `WorkloadSliceNameAnnotation` or falls
+  back to `WorkloadAnnotation` for non-elastic workloads.
+
+**Reconcile flow:**
+
+1. Skip workloads that are not admitted, finished, or evicted.
+2. List non-terminated pods for the workload slice via the field index (same lookup pattern
+   as `ElasticJobUngater.podsToUngate`).
+3. For each admitted `PodSet`, compare existing non-terminated pods (grouped by
+   `kueue.x-k8s.io/podset` label) against the granted counts on the Workload admission.
+4. For each required pod, check `PodScheduled=True` in the pod status conditions.
+5. Update the Workload `PodsReady` condition:
+   - If any required pod is not scheduled → `PodsReady=False`, reason `WaitForScheduling`.
+   - If all required pods are scheduled but not ready → delegate readiness to the job
+     framework (`generatePodsReadyCondition` / `PodsReady()`), which sets reason
+     `WaitForStart` or `WaitForRecovery` as today.
+6. If a pod list or index query fails, log the error and **do not** transition to
+   `WaitForScheduling` or evict; retry on the next reconcile.
```

  **Make timeout evaluation depend on fresh scheduling data.**
  
  If a list or index query fails, a persisted `WaitForScheduling` condition can remain stale while the workload controller's `unscheduledTimeout` path continues toward eviction. Add a freshness or error signal that suppresses eviction until scheduling state is observed successfully. Return the error or schedule an explicit requeue; logging alone does not guarantee a retry.

+
+## Summary
+
+Add `waitForPodsReady.unscheduledTimeout` to detect admitted workloads whose pods remain
+unscheduled (`PodScheduled != True`) sooner than the existing `timeout`, which is intended
+for post-schedule startup (image pull, init containers, readiness probes).
+
+## Motivation
+
+On on-premises clusters, pods may take a long time to become Ready after scheduling because
+of image pulling or initialization. Operators often set `waitForPodsReady.timeout` to 30
+minutes to accommodate that.
+
+However, pods that remain unscheduled due to timing or transient state inconsistencies
+between Kueue and the scheduler should be detected and requeued sooner. A separate timeout
+allows faster recovery without shortening the time allowed for normal pod startup.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Add a configurable timeout for admitted workloads with unscheduled pods.
+- Evict and requeue workloads that exceed the unscheduled timeout, reusing the existing
+  `waitForPodsReady` eviction and requeue machinery.
+- When `unscheduledTimeout` is enabled, start the `timeout` clock when all pods are scheduled,
+  so startup time is not consumed by scheduling delays.
+- Detect scheduling state centrally so custom in-house job integrations work without
+  per-integration code.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Replacing or modifying kube-scheduler scheduling queue timeouts.
+- Changing MultiKueue `PodScheduled` status sync behavior.
+- A separate eviction reason or requeue strategy; existing `PodsReadyTimeout` and
+  `requeuingStrategy` are reused.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Extend `WaitForPodsReady` with an optional `unscheduledTimeout` field. A new
+`UnscheduledPodsTracker` controller (following the TopologyUngater pattern) lists pods per
+admitted Workload, distinguishes unscheduled pods from scheduled-but-not-ready pods, and sets
+a new `PodsReady` condition reason `WaitForScheduling`. The workload controller enforces the
+shorter timeout when that reason is active and `unscheduledTimeout` is configured.
+
+### User Stories (Optional)
+
+#### Story 1
+
+An operator configures `waitForPodsReady.timeout: 30m` and `unscheduledTimeout: 5m`.
+A workload is admitted but its pods remain Pending because of a transient scheduler glitch.
+After 5 minutes Kueue evicts and requeues the workload. A pod that is scheduled but still
+pulling an image is allowed the full 30 minutes from the moment all pods are scheduled.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats (Optional)
+
+- `unscheduledTimeout` does not change kube-scheduler behavior or MultiKueue `PodScheduled`
+  status sync.
+- For `WaitForStart` when `unscheduledTimeout` is set, the startup timer uses reconciliation
+  observation time (`PodsReady.LastTransitionTime` when the reason changes), not the latest
+  pod `PodScheduled=True` transition.
+- When a running workload loses a scheduled pod, `WaitForRecovery` and `recoveryTimeout`
+  take precedence over `WaitForScheduling`.
+- `UnscheduledPodsTracker` and the job framework must coordinate updates to the `PodsReady`
+  condition so scheduling and readiness state do not conflict.

+# KEP-13502: Unscheduled Pods Timeout
+
+<!-- toc -->
+- [Summary](#summary)
+- [Motivation](#motivation)
+  - [Goals](#goals)
+  - [Non-Goals](#non-goals)
+- [Proposal](#proposal)
+  - [User Stories (Optional)](#user-stories-optional)
+    - [Story 1](#story-1)
+  - [Notes/Constraints/Caveats (Optional)](#notesconstraintscaveats-optional)
+  - [Risks and Mitigations](#risks-and-mitigations)
+- [Design Details](#design-details)
+  - [Kueue Configuration API](#kueue-configuration-api)
+  - [UnscheduledPodsTracker controller](#unscheduledpodstracker-controller)
+  - [Workload PodsReady condition](#workload-podsready-condition)
+  - [Timeout interaction](#timeout-interaction)
+  - [Eviction and requeue](#eviction-and-requeue)
+  - [Test Plan](#test-plan)
+    - [Prerequisite testing updates](#prerequisite-testing-updates)
+    - [Unit tests](#unit-tests)
+    - [Integration tests](#integration-tests)
+    - [e2e tests](#e2e-tests)
+  - [Graduation Criteria](#graduation-criteria)
+  - [Backward compatibility](#backward-compatibility)
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)
+- [Drawbacks](#drawbacks)
+- [Alternatives](#alternatives)
+  - [Per-integration <code>PodsScheduled</code> on <code>GenericJob</code>](#per-integration-podsscheduled-on-genericjob)
+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+Add `waitForPodsReady.unscheduledTimeout` to detect admitted workloads whose pods remain
+unscheduled (`PodScheduled != True`) sooner than the existing `timeout`, which is intended
+for post-schedule startup (image pull, init containers, readiness probes).
+
+## Motivation
+
+On on-premises clusters, pods may take a long time to become Ready after scheduling because
+of image pulling or initialization. Operators often set `waitForPodsReady.timeout` to 30
+minutes to accommodate that.
+
+However, pods that remain unscheduled due to timing or transient state inconsistencies
+between Kueue and the scheduler should be detected and requeued sooner. A separate timeout
+allows faster recovery without shortening the time allowed for normal pod startup.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Add a configurable timeout for admitted workloads with unscheduled pods.
+- Evict and requeue workloads that exceed the unscheduled timeout, reusing the existing
+  `waitForPodsReady` eviction and requeue machinery.
+- When `unscheduledTimeout` is enabled, start the `timeout` clock when all pods are scheduled,
+  so startup time is not consumed by scheduling delays.
+- Detect scheduling state centrally so custom in-house job integrations work without
+  per-integration code.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Replacing or modifying kube-scheduler scheduling queue timeouts.
+- Changing MultiKueue `PodScheduled` status sync behavior.
+- A separate eviction reason or requeue strategy; existing `PodsReadyTimeout` and
+  `requeuingStrategy` are reused.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Extend `WaitForPodsReady` with an optional `unscheduledTimeout` field. A new
+`UnscheduledPodsTracker` controller (following the TopologyUngater pattern) lists pods per
+admitted Workload, distinguishes unscheduled pods from scheduled-but-not-ready pods, and sets
+a new `PodsReady` condition reason `WaitForScheduling`. The workload controller enforces the
+shorter timeout when that reason is active and `unscheduledTimeout` is configured.
+
+### User Stories (Optional)
+
+#### Story 1
+
+An operator configures `waitForPodsReady.timeout: 30m` and `unscheduledTimeout: 5m`.
+A workload is admitted but its pods remain Pending because of a transient scheduler glitch.
+After 5 minutes Kueue evicts and requeues the workload. A pod that is scheduled but still
+pulling an image is allowed the full 30 minutes from the moment all pods are scheduled.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats (Optional)
+
+- `unscheduledTimeout` does not change kube-scheduler behavior or MultiKueue `PodScheduled`
+  status sync.
+- For `WaitForStart` when `unscheduledTimeout` is set, the startup timer uses reconciliation
+  observation time (`PodsReady.LastTransitionTime` when the reason changes), not the latest
+  pod `PodScheduled=True` transition.
+- When a running workload loses a scheduled pod, `WaitForRecovery` and `recoveryTimeout`
+  take precedence over `WaitForScheduling`.
+- `UnscheduledPodsTracker` and the job framework must coordinate updates to the `PodsReady`
+  condition so scheduling and readiness state do not conflict.
+
+### Risks and Mitigations
+
+| Risk | Mitigation |
+|------|------------|
+| Evict a healthy workload on a transient list/index error | Do not set `WaitForScheduling` or evict on client errors; retry on the next reconcile |
+| Increased requeue churn when `unscheduledTimeout` is too low | Operator tuning; existing `requeuingStrategy` backoff |
+| Consumers break on the new `WaitForScheduling` reason | Document in backward compatibility; reason is set even when `unscheduledTimeout` is disabled |
+| Controller and job framework both update `PodsReady` | Clear ownership: tracker reports scheduling; job framework reports readiness |
+
+## Design Details
+
+### Kueue Configuration API
+
+```yaml
+waitForPodsReady:
+  timeout: 30m
+  unscheduledTimeout: 5m
+```
+
+```go
+type WaitForPodsReady struct {
+    // ...
+    // UnscheduledTimeout defines the time for an admitted workload to have all
+    // required pods reach PodScheduled=True. When exceeded, the workload is evicted
+    // and requeued. Must be non-negative and must not exceed timeout.
+    // Defaults to disabled when unset or "0s".
+    // +optional
+    UnscheduledTimeout *metav1.Duration `json:"unscheduledTimeout,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+### UnscheduledPodsTracker controller
+
+A new controller, `UnscheduledPodsTracker`, follows the same architectural pattern as
+`TopologyUngater` ([KEP-2724](keps/2724-topology-aware-scheduling/README.md)) and
+`ElasticJobUngater`:
+
+- Reconciles per **Workload** (not per Job integration).
+- Watches **Pod** create/update/delete events and batches reconcile requests (using
+  `UpdatesBatchPeriod`, as TopologyUngater does).
+- Lists pods for a workload using the existing pod field index
+  (`WorkloadSliceNameKey` / `IndexPodWorkloadSliceName` in
+  `pkg/controller/core/indexer`), which indexes by `WorkloadSliceNameAnnotation` or falls
+  back to `WorkloadAnnotation` for non-elastic workloads.
+
+**Reconcile flow:**
+
+1. Skip workloads that are not admitted, finished, or evicted.
+2. List non-terminated pods for the workload slice via the field index (same lookup pattern
+   as `ElasticJobUngater.podsToUngate`).
+3. For each admitted `PodSet`, compare existing non-terminated pods (grouped by
+   `kueue.x-k8s.io/podset` label) against the granted counts on the Workload admission.
+4. For each required pod, check `PodScheduled=True` in the pod status conditions.
+5. Update the Workload `PodsReady` condition:
+   - If any required pod is not scheduled → `PodsReady=False`, reason `WaitForScheduling`.
+   - If all required pods are scheduled but not ready → delegate readiness to the job
+     framework (`generatePodsReadyCondition` / `PodsReady()`), which sets reason
+     `WaitForStart` or `WaitForRecovery` as today.
+6. If a pod list or index query fails, log the error and **do not** transition to
+   `WaitForScheduling` or evict; retry on the next reconcile.
```

+
+1. Skip workloads that are not admitted, finished, or evicted.
+2. List non-terminated pods for the workload slice via the field index (same lookup pattern
+   as `ElasticJobUngater.podsToUngate`).
+3. For each admitted `PodSet`, compare existing non-terminated pods (grouped by
+   `kueue.x-k8s.io/podset` label) against the granted counts on the Workload admission.
+4. For each required pod, check `PodScheduled=True` in the pod status conditions.
+5. Update the Workload `PodsReady` condition:
+   - If any required pod is not scheduled → `PodsReady=False`, reason `WaitForScheduling`.
+   - If all required pods are scheduled but not ready → delegate readiness to the job
+     framework (`generatePodsReadyCondition` / `PodsReady()`), which sets reason
+     `WaitForStart` or `WaitForRecovery` as today.
+6. If a pod list or index query fails, log the error and **do not** transition to
+   `WaitForScheduling` or evict; retry on the next reconcile.
```

@@ -0,0 +1,339 @@
+# KEP-13502: Unscheduled Pods Timeout
+
+<!-- toc -->
+- [Summary](#summary)
+- [Motivation](#motivation)
+  - [Goals](#goals)
+  - [Non-Goals](#non-goals)
+- [Proposal](#proposal)
+  - [User Stories (Optional)](#user-stories-optional)
+    - [Story 1](#story-1)
+  - [Notes/Constraints/Caveats (Optional)](#notesconstraintscaveats-optional)
+  - [Risks and Mitigations](#risks-and-mitigations)
+- [Design Details](#design-details)
+  - [Kueue Configuration API](#kueue-configuration-api)
+  - [UnscheduledPodsTracker controller](#unscheduledpodstracker-controller)
+  - [PodsReady condition ownership](#podsready-condition-ownership)
+  - [Workload PodsReady condition](#workload-podsready-condition)
+  - [Timeout interaction](#timeout-interaction)
+  - [Eviction and requeue](#eviction-and-requeue)
+  - [Test Plan](#test-plan)
+    - [Prerequisite testing updates](#prerequisite-testing-updates)
+    - [Unit tests](#unit-tests)
+    - [Integration tests](#integration-tests)
+    - [e2e tests](#e2e-tests)
+  - [Graduation Criteria](#graduation-criteria)
+  - [Backward compatibility](#backward-compatibility)
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)
+- [Drawbacks](#drawbacks)
+- [Alternatives](#alternatives)
+  - [Per-integration <code>PodsScheduled</code> on <code>GenericJob</code>](#per-integration-podsscheduled-on-genericjob)
+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+Add `waitForPodsReady.unscheduledTimeout` to detect admitted workloads whose pods remain
+unscheduled (`PodScheduled != True`) sooner than the existing `timeout`, which is intended
+for post-schedule startup (image pull, init containers, readiness probes).
+
+## Motivation
+
+On on-premises clusters, pods may take a long time to become Ready after scheduling because
+of image pulling or initialization. Operators often set `waitForPodsReady.timeout` to 30
+minutes to accommodate that.
+
+However, pods that remain unscheduled due to timing or transient state inconsistencies
+between Kueue and the scheduler should be detected and requeued sooner. A separate timeout
+allows faster recovery without shortening the time allowed for normal pod startup.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Add a configurable timeout for admitted workloads with unscheduled pods.
+- Evict and requeue workloads that exceed the unscheduled timeout, reusing the existing
+  `waitForPodsReady` eviction and requeue machinery.
+- When `unscheduledTimeout` is enabled, start the `timeout` clock when all pods are scheduled,
+  so startup time is not consumed by scheduling delays.
+- Detect scheduling state centrally so custom in-house job integrations work without
+  per-integration code.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Replacing or modifying kube-scheduler scheduling queue timeouts.
+- Changing MultiKueue `PodScheduled` status sync behavior.
+- A separate eviction reason or requeue strategy; existing `PodsReadyTimeout` and
+  `requeuingStrategy` are reused.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Extend `WaitForPodsReady` with an optional `unscheduledTimeout` field. A new
+`UnscheduledPodsTracker` controller (following the TopologyUngater pattern) lists pods per
+admitted Workload, distinguishes unscheduled pods from scheduled-but-not-ready pods, and sets
+a new `PodsReady` condition reason `WaitForScheduling`. The workload controller enforces the
+shorter timeout when that reason is active and `unscheduledTimeout` is configured.
+
+### User Stories (Optional)
+
+#### Story 1
+
+An operator configures `waitForPodsReady.timeout: 30m` and `unscheduledTimeout: 5m`.
+A workload is admitted but its pods remain Pending because of a transient scheduler glitch.
+After 5 minutes Kueue evicts and requeues the workload. A pod that is scheduled but still
+pulling an image is allowed the full 30 minutes from the moment all pods are scheduled.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats (Optional)
+
+- `unscheduledTimeout` does not change kube-scheduler behavior or MultiKueue `PodScheduled`
+  status sync.
+- For `WaitForStart` when `unscheduledTimeout` is set, the startup timer uses reconciliation
+  observation time (`PodsReady.LastTransitionTime` when the reason changes), not the latest
+  pod `PodScheduled=True` transition.
+- When a running workload loses a scheduled pod, `WaitForRecovery` and `recoveryTimeout`
+  take precedence over `WaitForScheduling`.
+- Single owner for scheduling-related `PodsReady` updates: see
+  [PodsReady condition ownership](#podsready-condition-ownership). The job framework handles
+  readiness only; it does not call per-integration scheduling probes in the primary design.
+
```

@@ -0,0 +1,339 @@
+# KEP-13502: Unscheduled Pods Timeout
+
+<!-- toc -->
+- [Summary](#summary)
+- [Motivation](#motivation)
+  - [Goals](#goals)
+  - [Non-Goals](#non-goals)
+- [Proposal](#proposal)
+  - [User Stories (Optional)](#user-stories-optional)
+    - [Story 1](#story-1)
+  - [Notes/Constraints/Caveats (Optional)](#notesconstraintscaveats-optional)
+  - [Risks and Mitigations](#risks-and-mitigations)
+- [Design Details](#design-details)
+  - [Kueue Configuration API](#kueue-configuration-api)
+  - [UnscheduledPodsTracker controller](#unscheduledpodstracker-controller)
+  - [PodsReady condition ownership](#podsready-condition-ownership)
+  - [Workload PodsReady condition](#workload-podsready-condition)
+  - [Timeout interaction](#timeout-interaction)
+  - [Eviction and requeue](#eviction-and-requeue)

+    - [Prerequisite testing updates](#prerequisite-testing-updates)

+  - [Graduation Criteria](#graduation-criteria)
+  - [Backward compatibility](#backward-compatibility)
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)
+- [Drawbacks](#drawbacks)
+- [Alternatives](#alternatives)
+  - [Per-integration <code>PodsScheduled</code> on <code>GenericJob</code>](#per-integration-podsscheduled-on-genericjob)
+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+Add `waitForPodsReady.unscheduledTimeout` to detect admitted workloads whose pods remain
+unscheduled (`PodScheduled != True`) sooner than the existing `timeout`, which is intended
+for post-schedule startup (image pull, init containers, readiness probes).
+
+## Motivation
+
+On on-premises clusters, pods may take a long time to become Ready after scheduling because
+of image pulling or initialization. Operators often set `waitForPodsReady.timeout` to 30
+minutes to accommodate that.
+
+However, pods that remain unscheduled due to timing or transient state inconsistencies
+between Kueue and the scheduler should be detected and requeued sooner. A separate timeout
+allows faster recovery without shortening the time allowed for normal pod startup.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Add a configurable timeout for admitted workloads with unscheduled pods.
+- Evict and requeue workloads that exceed the unscheduled timeout, reusing the existing
+  `waitForPodsReady` eviction and requeue machinery.
+- When `unscheduledTimeout` is enabled, start the `timeout` clock when all pods are scheduled,
+  so startup time is not consumed by scheduling delays.
+- Detect scheduling state centrally so custom in-house job integrations work without
+  per-integration code.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Replacing or modifying kube-scheduler scheduling queue timeouts.
+- Changing MultiKueue `PodScheduled` status sync behavior.
+- A separate eviction reason or requeue strategy; existing `PodsReadyTimeout` and
+  `requeuingStrategy` are reused.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Extend `WaitForPodsReady` with an optional `unscheduledTimeout` field. A new
+`UnscheduledPodsTracker` controller (following the TopologyUngater pattern) lists pods per
+admitted Workload, distinguishes unscheduled pods from scheduled-but-not-ready pods, and sets
+a new `PodsReady` condition reason `WaitForScheduling`. The workload controller enforces the
+shorter timeout when that reason is active and `unscheduledTimeout` is configured.
+
+### User Stories (Optional)
+
+#### Story 1
+
+An operator configures `waitForPodsReady.timeout: 30m` and `unscheduledTimeout: 5m`.
+A workload is admitted but its pods remain Pending because of a transient scheduler glitch.
+After 5 minutes Kueue evicts and requeues the workload. A pod that is scheduled but still
+pulling an image is allowed the full 30 minutes from the moment all pods are scheduled.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats (Optional)
+
+- `unscheduledTimeout` does not change kube-scheduler behavior or MultiKueue `PodScheduled`
+  status sync.
+- For `WaitForStart` when `unscheduledTimeout` is set, the startup timer uses reconciliation
+  observation time (`PodsReady.LastTransitionTime` when the reason changes), not the latest
+  pod `PodScheduled=True` transition.
+- When a running workload loses a scheduled pod, `WaitForRecovery` and `recoveryTimeout`
+  take precedence over `WaitForScheduling`.
+- Single owner for scheduling-related `PodsReady` updates: see
+  [PodsReady condition ownership](#podsready-condition-ownership). The job framework handles
+  readiness only; it does not call per-integration scheduling probes in the primary design.
+
+### Risks and Mitigations
+
+| Risk | Mitigation |
+|------|------------|
+| Evict a healthy workload on a transient list/index error | Return error (requeue); set `SchedulingObserved=False` with reason `ObservationFailed`; workload controller suppresses `unscheduledTimeout` eviction until observation succeeds |
+| Increased requeue churn when `unscheduledTimeout` is too low | Operator tuning; existing `requeuingStrategy` backoff |
+| Consumers break on the new `WaitForScheduling` reason | Document in backward compatibility; reason is set even when `unscheduledTimeout` is disabled |
+| Controller and job framework both update `PodsReady` | See [PodsReady condition ownership](#podsready-condition-ownership); tracker owns scheduling state and `SchedulingObserved`; job framework owns readiness only |
+
+## Design Details
+
+### Kueue Configuration API
+
+```yaml
+waitForPodsReady:
+  timeout: 30m
+  unscheduledTimeout: 5m
+```
+
+```go
+type WaitForPodsReady struct {
+    // ...
+    // UnscheduledTimeout defines the time for an admitted workload to have all
+    // required pods reach PodScheduled=True. When exceeded, the workload is evicted
+    // and requeued. Must be non-negative and must not exceed timeout.
+    // Defaults to disabled when unset or "0s".
+    // +optional
+    UnscheduledTimeout *metav1.Duration `json:"unscheduledTimeout,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+### UnscheduledPodsTracker controller
+
+A new controller, `UnscheduledPodsTracker`, follows the same architectural pattern as
+`TopologyUngater` ([KEP-2724](keps/2724-topology-aware-scheduling/README.md)) and
+`ElasticJobUngater`:
+
+- Reconciles per **Workload** (not per Job integration).
+- Watches **Pod** create/update/delete events and batches reconcile requests (using
+  `UpdatesBatchPeriod`, as TopologyUngater does).
+- Lists pods for a workload using the existing pod field index
+  (`WorkloadSliceNameKey` / `IndexPodWorkloadSliceName` in
+  `pkg/controller/core/indexer`), which indexes by `WorkloadSliceNameAnnotation` or falls
+  back to `WorkloadAnnotation` for non-elastic workloads.
+
+**Reconcile flow:**
+
+1. Skip workloads that are not admitted, finished, or evicted.
+2. List non-terminated pods for the workload slice via the field index (same lookup pattern
+   as `ElasticJobUngater.podsToUngate`).
+3. For each admitted `PodSet`, compare existing non-terminated pods (grouped by
+   `kueue.x-k8s.io/podset` label) against the granted counts on the Workload admission.
+4. For each required pod, check `PodScheduled=True` in the pod status conditions.
+5. On a successful pod list/index query, set `SchedulingObserved=True` and update the
+   Workload `PodsReady` condition:
+   - If any required pod is not scheduled → `PodsReady=False`, reason `WaitForScheduling`.
+   - If all required pods are scheduled but not ready → stop asserting scheduling state;
+     delegate readiness to the job framework (`generatePodsReadyCondition` / `PodsReady()`),
+     which sets reason `WaitForStart` or `WaitForRecovery` as today.
+6. If a pod list or index query fails, return an error (requeue), set
+   `SchedulingObserved=False` with reason `ObservationFailed`, and **do not** infer
+   `WaitForScheduling` from failed data. The workload controller suppresses
+   `unscheduledTimeout` eviction while `SchedulingObserved` is not `True`.
+
+**Required-pod semantics:**
+
+| Case | Behavior |
+|------|----------|
+| Pod not yet created (count below granted) | Treat as not all scheduled; remain in or enter `WaitForScheduling`. |
+| Terminated pod (Succeeded/Failed) | Excluded from the scheduling check (same as TopologyUngater). |
+| Pod deleted or preempted while workload was running | `WaitForRecovery` takes precedence over `WaitForScheduling`; `recoveryTimeout` applies (see below). |
+| Optional PodSets with zero count | No pods required; scheduling check passes for that PodSet. |
+| List/index client error | `SchedulingObserved=False`, reason `ObservationFailed`; no eviction. |
+| Successful observe, pods unscheduled | `SchedulingObserved=True`; `WaitForScheduling`; eviction per timeout table. |
+| Successful observe, all scheduled | `SchedulingObserved=True`; hand off to job framework for `WaitForStart`. |
+
+### PodsReady condition ownership
+
+| Component | Responsibility |
+|-----------|----------------|
+| `UnscheduledPodsTracker` | Pod list/index, `PodScheduled` checks, `SchedulingObserved`, set `PodsReady=False` with reason `WaitForScheduling`, hand off when all pods are scheduled |
+| Job framework `generatePodsReadyCondition` | **Readiness only** via `GenericJob.PodsReady()` when `SchedulingObserved=True` and all required pods are scheduled |
+| Workload controller | Timeout and eviction math only; does not list pods |
+
+**Coordination rules:**
+
+1. The job reconciler does **not** call `PodsScheduled()` on `GenericJob` in the primary
+   design (no per-integration scheduling probes).
+2. When not all pods are scheduled, **only** `UnscheduledPodsTracker` sets reason
+   `WaitForScheduling`.
+3. When all pods are scheduled, the tracker stops asserting `WaitForScheduling`; the job
+   framework sets `WaitForStart` or `WaitForRecovery` via existing readiness logic.
+4. **Recovery precedence:** if `PodsReady` was `True` and a pod fails or is removed, the job
+   framework keeps reason `WaitForRecovery`; the tracker does not overwrite with
+   `WaitForScheduling` (see `generatePodsReadyCondition` in
+   `pkg/controller/jobframework/reconciler.go`).
+5. The implementation prototype in [#13614](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13614)
+   that calls `PodsScheduled()` inside `generatePodsReadyCondition` is **superseded** by this
+   KEP; [#13614](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13614) will pivot after the KEP
+   merges. Per-integration `PodsScheduled` remains documented under [Alternatives](#alternatives).
+
+### Workload PodsReady condition
+
+Add constants:
+
+```go
+WorkloadWaitForScheduling = "WaitForScheduling"
+// SchedulingObserved indicates UnscheduledPodsTracker successfully observed
+// required pods for scheduling state. False suppresses unscheduledTimeout eviction.
+WorkloadSchedulingObserved = "SchedulingObserved"
+```
```

@@ -0,0 +1,339 @@
+# KEP-13502: Unscheduled Pods Timeout
+
+<!-- toc -->
+- [Summary](#summary)
+- [Motivation](#motivation)
+  - [Goals](#goals)
+  - [Non-Goals](#non-goals)
+- [Proposal](#proposal)
+  - [User Stories (Optional)](#user-stories-optional)
+    - [Story 1](#story-1)
+  - [Notes/Constraints/Caveats (Optional)](#notesconstraintscaveats-optional)
+  - [Risks and Mitigations](#risks-and-mitigations)
+- [Design Details](#design-details)
+  - [Kueue Configuration API](#kueue-configuration-api)
+  - [UnscheduledPodsTracker controller](#unscheduledpodstracker-controller)
+  - [PodsReady condition ownership](#podsready-condition-ownership)
+  - [Workload PodsReady condition](#workload-podsready-condition)
+  - [Timeout interaction](#timeout-interaction)
+  - [Eviction and requeue](#eviction-and-requeue)
+  - [Test Plan](#test-plan)
+    - [Prerequisite testing updates](#prerequisite-testing-updates)
+    - [Unit tests](#unit-tests)
+    - [Integration tests](#integration-tests)
+    - [e2e tests](#e2e-tests)
+  - [Graduation Criteria](#graduation-criteria)
+  - [Backward compatibility](#backward-compatibility)
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)
+- [Drawbacks](#drawbacks)
+- [Alternatives](#alternatives)
+  - [Per-integration <code>PodsScheduled</code> on <code>GenericJob</code>](#per-integration-podsscheduled-on-genericjob)
+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+Add `waitForPodsReady.unscheduledTimeout` to detect admitted workloads whose pods remain
+unscheduled (`PodScheduled != True`) sooner than the existing `timeout`, which is intended
+for post-schedule startup (image pull, init containers, readiness probes).
+
+## Motivation
+
+On on-premises clusters, pods may take a long time to become Ready after scheduling because
+of image pulling or initialization. Operators often set `waitForPodsReady.timeout` to 30
+minutes to accommodate that.
+
+However, pods that remain unscheduled due to timing or transient state inconsistencies
+between Kueue and the scheduler should be detected and requeued sooner. A separate timeout
+allows faster recovery without shortening the time allowed for normal pod startup.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Add a configurable timeout for admitted workloads with unscheduled pods.
+- Evict and requeue workloads that exceed the unscheduled timeout, reusing the existing
+  `waitForPodsReady` eviction and requeue machinery.
+- When `unscheduledTimeout` is enabled, start the `timeout` clock when all pods are scheduled,
+  so startup time is not consumed by scheduling delays.
+- Detect scheduling state centrally so custom in-house job integrations work without
+  per-integration code.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Replacing or modifying kube-scheduler scheduling queue timeouts.
+- Changing MultiKueue `PodScheduled` status sync behavior.
+- A separate eviction reason or requeue strategy; existing `PodsReadyTimeout` and
+  `requeuingStrategy` are reused.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Extend `WaitForPodsReady` with an optional `unscheduledTimeout` field. A new
+`UnscheduledPodsTracker` controller (following the TopologyUngater pattern) lists pods per
+admitted Workload, distinguishes unscheduled pods from scheduled-but-not-ready pods, and sets
+a new `PodsReady` condition reason `WaitForScheduling`. The workload controller enforces the
+shorter timeout when that reason is active and `unscheduledTimeout` is configured.
+
+### User Stories (Optional)
+
+#### Story 1
+
+An operator configures `waitForPodsReady.timeout: 30m` and `unscheduledTimeout: 5m`.
+A workload is admitted but its pods remain Pending because of a transient scheduler glitch.
+After 5 minutes Kueue evicts and requeues the workload. A pod that is scheduled but still
+pulling an image is allowed the full 30 minutes from the moment all pods are scheduled.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats (Optional)
+
+- `unscheduledTimeout` does not change kube-scheduler behavior or MultiKueue `PodScheduled`
+  status sync.
+- For `WaitForStart` when `unscheduledTimeout` is set, the startup timer uses reconciliation
+  observation time (`PodsReady.LastTransitionTime` when the reason changes), not the latest
+  pod `PodScheduled=True` transition.
+- When a running workload loses a scheduled pod, `WaitForRecovery` and `recoveryTimeout`
+  take precedence over `WaitForScheduling`.
+- Single owner for scheduling-related `PodsReady` updates: see
+  [PodsReady condition ownership](#podsready-condition-ownership). The job framework handles
+  readiness only; it does not call per-integration scheduling probes in the primary design.
+
+### Risks and Mitigations
+
+| Risk | Mitigation |
+|------|------------|
+| Evict a healthy workload on a transient list/index error | Return error (requeue); set `SchedulingObserved=False` with reason `ObservationFailed`; workload controller suppresses `unscheduledTimeout` eviction until observation succeeds |
+| Increased requeue churn when `unscheduledTimeout` is too low | Operator tuning; existing `requeuingStrategy` backoff |
+| Consumers break on the new `WaitForScheduling` reason | Document in backward compatibility; reason is set even when `unscheduledTimeout` is disabled |
+| Controller and job framework both update `PodsReady` | See [PodsReady condition ownership](#podsready-condition-ownership); tracker owns scheduling state and `SchedulingObserved`; job framework owns readiness only |
+
+## Design Details
+
+### Kueue Configuration API
+
+```yaml
+waitForPodsReady:
+  timeout: 30m
+  unscheduledTimeout: 5m
+```
+
+```go
+type WaitForPodsReady struct {
+    // ...
+    // UnscheduledTimeout defines the time for an admitted workload to have all
+    // required pods reach PodScheduled=True. When exceeded, the workload is evicted
+    // and requeued. Must be non-negative and must not exceed timeout.
+    // Defaults to disabled when unset or "0s".
+    // +optional
+    UnscheduledTimeout *metav1.Duration `json:"unscheduledTimeout,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+### UnscheduledPodsTracker controller
+
+A new controller, `UnscheduledPodsTracker`, follows the same architectural pattern as
+`TopologyUngater` ([KEP-2724](keps/2724-topology-aware-scheduling/README.md)) and
+`ElasticJobUngater`:
+
+- Reconciles per **Workload** (not per Job integration).
+- Watches **Pod** create/update/delete events and batches reconcile requests (using
+  `UpdatesBatchPeriod`, as TopologyUngater does).
+- Lists pods for a workload using the existing pod field index
+  (`WorkloadSliceNameKey` / `IndexPodWorkloadSliceName` in
+  `pkg/controller/core/indexer`), which indexes by `WorkloadSliceNameAnnotation` or falls
+  back to `WorkloadAnnotation` for non-elastic workloads.
+
+**Reconcile flow:**
+
+1. Skip workloads that are not admitted, finished, or evicted.
+2. List non-terminated pods for the workload slice via the field index (same lookup pattern
+   as `ElasticJobUngater.podsToUngate`).
+3. For each admitted `PodSet`, compare existing non-terminated pods (grouped by
+   `kueue.x-k8s.io/podset` label) against the granted counts on the Workload admission.
+4. For each required pod, check `PodScheduled=True` in the pod status conditions.
+5. On a successful pod list/index query, set `SchedulingObserved=True` and update the
+   Workload `PodsReady` condition:
+   - If any required pod is not scheduled → `PodsReady=False`, reason `WaitForScheduling`.
+   - If all required pods are scheduled but not ready → stop asserting scheduling state;
+     delegate readiness to the job framework (`generatePodsReadyCondition` / `PodsReady()`),
+     which sets reason `WaitForStart` or `WaitForRecovery` as today.
+6. If a pod list or index query fails, return an error (requeue), set
+   `SchedulingObserved=False` with reason `ObservationFailed`, and **do not** infer
+   `WaitForScheduling` from failed data. The workload controller suppresses
+   `unscheduledTimeout` eviction while `SchedulingObserved` is not `True`.
+
+**Required-pod semantics:**
+
+| Case | Behavior |
+|------|----------|
+| Pod not yet created (count below granted) | Treat as not all scheduled; remain in or enter `WaitForScheduling`. |
+| Terminated pod (Succeeded/Failed) | Excluded from the scheduling check (same as TopologyUngater). |
+| Pod deleted or preempted while workload was running | `WaitForRecovery` takes precedence over `WaitForScheduling`; `recoveryTimeout` applies (see below). |
+| Optional PodSets with zero count | No pods required; scheduling check passes for that PodSet. |
+| List/index client error | `SchedulingObserved=False`, reason `ObservationFailed`; no eviction. |
+| Successful observe, pods unscheduled | `SchedulingObserved=True`; `WaitForScheduling`; eviction per timeout table. |
+| Successful observe, all scheduled | `SchedulingObserved=True`; hand off to job framework for `WaitForStart`. |
+
+### PodsReady condition ownership
+
+| Component | Responsibility |
+|-----------|----------------|
+| `UnscheduledPodsTracker` | Pod list/index, `PodScheduled` checks, `SchedulingObserved`, set `PodsReady=False` with reason `WaitForScheduling`, hand off when all pods are scheduled |
+| Job framework `generatePodsReadyCondition` | **Readiness only** via `GenericJob.PodsReady()` when `SchedulingObserved=True` and all required pods are scheduled |
+| Workload controller | Timeout and eviction math only; does not list pods |
+
+**Coordination rules:**
+
+1. The job reconciler does **not** call `PodsScheduled()` on `GenericJob` in the primary
+   design (no per-integration scheduling probes).
+2. When not all pods are scheduled, **only** `UnscheduledPodsTracker` sets reason
+   `WaitForScheduling`.
+3. When all pods are scheduled, the tracker stops asserting `WaitForScheduling`; the job
+   framework sets `WaitForStart` or `WaitForRecovery` via existing readiness logic.
+4. **Recovery precedence:** if `PodsReady` was `True` and a pod fails or is removed, the job
+   framework keeps reason `WaitForRecovery`; the tracker does not overwrite with
+   `WaitForScheduling` (see `generatePodsReadyCondition` in
+   `pkg/controller/jobframework/reconciler.go`).
+5. The implementation prototype in [#13614](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13614)
+   that calls `PodsScheduled()` inside `generatePodsReadyCondition` is **superseded** by this
+   KEP; [#13614](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13614) will pivot after the KEP
+   merges. Per-integration `PodsScheduled` remains documented under [Alternatives](#alternatives).
+
+### Workload PodsReady condition
+
+Add constants:
+
+```go
+WorkloadWaitForScheduling = "WaitForScheduling"
+// SchedulingObserved indicates UnscheduledPodsTracker successfully observed
+// required pods for scheduling state. False suppresses unscheduledTimeout eviction.
+WorkloadSchedulingObserved = "SchedulingObserved"
+```
+
+`UnscheduledPodsTracker` sets `PodsReady=False` with reason `WaitForScheduling` when not
+all required pods have `PodScheduled=True`. When all required pods are scheduled but not
+ready, the job framework sets `WaitForStart` (or `WaitForRecovery` after a failure) via
+the existing `generatePodsReadyCondition` path.
+
+**Recovery precedence:** When a workload was `PodsReady=True` and a required pod fails or
+is removed, the job framework keeps reason `WaitForRecovery` (not `WaitForScheduling`).
+`recoveryTimeout` remains authoritative for that transition. `WaitForScheduling` applies
+only on the initial scheduling path after admission, not when recovering from a running
+workload failure.
+
+### Timeout interaction
+
+| `PodsReady` reason | `unscheduledTimeout` | Timer start | Duration |
+|--------------------|----------------------|-------------|----------|
+| `WaitForScheduling` | not set / `0s` | `WorkloadAdmitted` | `timeout` (backward compatible) |
+| `WaitForScheduling` | set | `WorkloadAdmitted` | `unscheduledTimeout` (only when `SchedulingObserved=True`) |
+| `SchedulingObserved=False` | set | — | No eviction deadline; tracker requeue until observation succeeds |
+| `WaitForStart` | set | `PodsReady.LastTransitionTime` | `timeout` |
+| `WaitForStart` | not set | `WorkloadAdmitted` | `timeout` (unchanged) |
+| `WaitForRecovery` | any | `PodsReady.LastTransitionTime` | `recoveryTimeout` (unchanged) |
+
+For the `WaitForStart` row when `unscheduledTimeout` is set, `PodsReady.LastTransitionTime`
+is the **reconciliation observation time** when the reason transitions from
+`WaitForScheduling` to `WaitForStart` (consistent with today's
+`generatePodsReadyCondition` clock). Delayed reconciliation can therefore extend the
+startup window slightly beyond the latest pod `PodScheduled=True` transition.
+
+When `unscheduledTimeout` is not configured, `WaitForScheduling` is still set for
+observability but timeout math is unchanged from today.
+
+### Eviction and requeue
+
+Eviction uses `WorkloadEvictedByPodsReadyTimeout` with underlying cause
+`WorkloadWaitForScheduling`. Requeue backoff uses the existing `requeuingStrategy`.
+
+In `admittedNotReadyWorkload` (`pkg/controller/core/workload_controller.go`), extend timeout
+math so `unscheduledTimeout` eviction for reason `WaitForScheduling` runs only when
+`SchedulingObserved=True`. When `SchedulingObserved=False` (for example after a pod
+list/index error), return no eviction deadline and rely on the tracker to requeue and
+restore observation.
+
+### Test Plan
+
+[x] I/we understand the owners of the involved components may require updates to
+existing tests to make this code solid enough prior to committing the changes necessary
+to implement this enhancement.
+
+#### Prerequisite testing updates
+
+Existing integration coverage for `waitForPodsReady` in job controller integration tests
+and workload controller unit tests in `pkg/controller/core/workload_controller_test.go`
+provide the foundation for this enhancement.
+
+#### Unit tests
+
+- `UnscheduledPodsTracker`: pod index listing, required-pod counting per PodSet,
+  `PodScheduled` evaluation, `SchedulingObserved` transitions, list/index errors,
+  terminated-pod exclusion, and condition transitions (`WaitForScheduling` ↔ `WaitForStart`).
+- Workload controller: timeout math with `WaitForScheduling` reason and
+  `unscheduledTimeout` boundary values (unset, `0s`, equal to `timeout`; greater than
+  `timeout` rejected at validation).
```

  _🎯 Functional Correctness_ | _🟡 Minor_ | _⚡ Quick win_
  
  **Add a negative-value validation test.**
  
  The API contract requires non-negative `unscheduledTimeout`, but the test plan lists only unset, `0s`, equal, and greater-than-`timeout` cases. Add an explicit negative-duration case and verify that validation rejects it.
